control)\">Repository</a></th><td class=\"infobox-data\"><span class=\"url\"><a rel=\"mw:ExtLink nofollow\" href=\"https://git.cgsecurity.org/cgit/testdisk/\" class=\"external text\">git<wbr>.cgsecurity<wbr>.org<wbr>/cgit<wbr>/testdisk<wbr>/</a></span></td></tr></tbody></table><span about=\"#mwt4\"> </span>\n<p id=\"mwBQ\"><b id=\"mwBg\">PhotoRec</b> is a <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_and_open-source\" title=\"Free and open-source\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwBw\">free and open-source</a> <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Utility_software\" title=\"Utility software\" id=\"mwCA\">utility software</a> for <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_recovery\" title=\"Data recovery\" id=\"mwCQ\">data recovery</a> with <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Text-based_user_interface\" title=\"Text-based user interface\" id=\"mwCg\">text-based user interface</a> using <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_carving\" title=\"Data carving\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwCw\">data carving</a> techniques, designed to recover lost files from various digital camera memory, <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hard_disk\" title=\"Hard disk\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwDA\">hard disk</a> and <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CD-ROM\" title=\"CD-ROM\" id=\"mwDQ\">CD-ROM</a>. It can recover the files with more than 480 file extensions (about 300 file families).<sup about=\"#mwt9\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-FileFormats_1-0\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;FileFormats&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-FileFormats-1&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-FileFormats-1\" id=\"mwDg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwDw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwEA\">[</span>1<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwEQ\">]</span></span></a></sup> It is also possible to add custom <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_magic_number\" title=\"File magic number\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwEg\">file signature</a> to detect less known files.<sup about=\"#mwt12\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-2\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-2&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-2\" id=\"mwEw\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwFA\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwFQ\">[</span>2<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwFg\">]</span></span></a></sup></p>\n\n<p id=\"mwFw\">PhotoRec does not attempt to write to the damaged media the user is about to recover from. Recovered files are instead written to the directory from which PhotoRec is run, any other directory may be chosen. It can be used for <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_recovery\" title=\"Data recovery\" id=\"mwGA\">data recovery</a> or in a <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Digital_forensics\" title=\"Digital forensics\" id=\"mwGQ\">digital forensics</a> context.<sup about=\"#mwt15\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-3\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-3&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-3\" id=\"mwGg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwGw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwHA\">[</span>3<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwHQ\">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about=\"#mwt18\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-4\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-4&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-4\" id=\"mwHg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwHw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwIA\">[</span>4<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwIQ\">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about=\"#mwt21\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-5\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-5&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-5\" id=\"mwIg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwIw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwJA\">[</span>5<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwJQ\">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about=\"#mwt23\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-GraphicFileCarvingTool_6-0\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;GraphicFileCarvingTool&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-GraphicFileCarvingTool-6&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-GraphicFileCarvingTool-6\" id=\"mwJg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwJw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwKA\">[</span>6<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwKQ\">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about=\"#mwt25\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-VideoFileCarvingTool_7-0\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;VideoFileCarvingTool&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-VideoFileCarvingTool-7&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-VideoFileCarvingTool-7\" id=\"mwKg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwKw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwLA\">[</span>7<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwLQ\">]</span></span></a></sup>  PhotoRec is shipped with <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TestDisk\" title=\"TestDisk\" id=\"mwLg\">TestDisk</a>.<sup about=\"#mwt28\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-8\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-8&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-8\" id=\"mwLw\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwMA\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwMQ\">[</span>8<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwMg\">]</span></span></a></sup></p>\n\n<meta property=\"mw:PageProp/toc\" id=\"mwMw\" data-mw=\"{&quot;autoGenerated&quot;:true}\"></section><section data-mw-section-id=\"1\" id=\"mwNA\" aria-labelledby=\"Functionality\"><div class=\"mw-heading mw-heading2\"><h2 id=\"Functionality\">Functionality</h2><span class=\"mw-editsection\"><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">[</span><a href=\"/w/index.php?title=PhotoRec&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1\" title=\"Edit section: Functionality\"><span>edit</span></a><span class=\"mw-editsection-bracket\">]</span></span></div>\n<p id=\"mwNQ\"><a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_Allocation_Table\" title=\"File Allocation Table\" id=\"mwNg\">FAT</a>, <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Technology_File_System\" title=\"New Technology File System\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwNw\">NTFS</a>, <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ext2\" title=\"Ext2\" id=\"mwOA\">ext2</a>/<a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ext3\" title=\"Ext3\" id=\"mwOQ\">ext3</a>/<a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ext4\" title=\"Ext4\" id=\"mwOg\">ext4</a> <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_system\" title=\"File system\" id=\"mwOw\">file systems</a> store files in data blocks (also called <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Data_cluster\" title=\"Data cluster\" class=\"mw-redirect\" id=\"mwPA\">data clusters</a> under Windows). The cluster or block size remains at a constant number of sectors after being initialized during the formatting of the filesystem. In general, most operating systems try to store the data in a contiguous way so as to minimize <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fragmentation_(computing)\" title=\"Fragmentation (computing)\" id=\"mwPQ\">data fragmentation</a>. The seek time of mechanical drives is significant for writing and reading data to/from a hard disk, so that is why it is important to keep the fragmentation to a minimum level.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwPg\">When a file is deleted, the meta-information about this file (filename, date/time, size, location of the first data block/cluster, etc.) is lost; e.g., in an ext3/ext4 filesystem, the names of deleted files are still present, but the location of the first data block is removed. This means the data is still present on the filesystem, but only until some or all of it is overwritten by new file data.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwPw\">To recover these \"lost\" files, PhotoRec first tries to find the data block (or cluster) size. If the filesystem is not corrupted, this value can be read from the superblock (ext2/ext3/ext4) or volume boot record (FAT, NTFS). Otherwise, PhotoRec reads the media, sector by sector, searching for the first ten files, from which it calculates the block/cluster size from their locations. Once this block size is known, PhotoRec reads the media block by block (or cluster by cluster). Each block is checked against a signature database; which comes with the program and has been growing in the type of files it can recover ever since PhotoRec's first version came out. It is a common data recovery method called <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File_carving\" title=\"File carving\" id=\"mwQA\">file carving</a>.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwQQ\">For example, PhotoRec identifies a <a rel=\"mw:WikiLink\" href=\"https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/JPEG\" title=\"JPEG\" id=\"mwQg\">JPEG</a> file when a block begins with:</p>\n<ul id=\"mwQw\"><li id=\"mwRA\">Start Of Image + APP0: 0xff, 0xd8, 0xff, 0xe0</li>\n<li id=\"mwRQ\">Start Of Image + APP1: 0xff, 0xd8, 0xff, 0xe1</li>\n<li id=\"mwRg\">or Start Of Image + Comment: 0xff, 0xd8, 0xff, 0xfe</li></ul>\n\n<p id=\"mwRw\">If PhotoRec has already started to recover a file, it stops its recovery, checks the consistency of the file when possible and starts to save the new file (which it determined from the signature it found).</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwSA\">If the data is not fragmented, the recovered file should be identical to (or possibly larger than) the original file in size. In some cases, PhotoRec can learn the original file size from the file header, so the recovered file is truncated to the correct size. If, however, the recovered file ends up being smaller than its header specifies, it is discarded. Some files, such as *.MP3 types, are data streams. In this case, PhotoRec parses the recovered data, then stops the recovery when the stream ends.</p>\n\n<p id=\"mwSQ\">When a file is recovered successfully, PhotoRec checks the previous data blocks to see whether a file signature was found but the file was not able to be successfully recovered (i.e., the file was too small), and it tries again. This way, some fragmented files can be successfully recovered.<sup about=\"#mwt29\" class=\"mw-ref reference\" id=\"cite_ref-PhotoRec_9-0\" rel=\"dc:references\" typeof=\"mw:Extension/ref\" data-mw=\"{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;ref&quot;,&quot;attrs&quot;:{&quot;name&quot;:&quot;PhotoRec&quot;},&quot;body&quot;:{&quot;id&quot;:&quot;mw-reference-text-cite_note-PhotoRec-9&quot;}}\"><a href=\"#cite_note-PhotoRec-9\" id=\"mwSg\"><span class=\"mw-reflink-text\" id=\"mwSw\"><span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwTA\">[</span>9<span class=\"cite-bracket\" id=\"mwTQ\">]</span></span></a></sup></p>\n\n</section><section data-mw-section-id=\"2\" id=\"mwTg\" aria-labelledby=\"Compatibility\"><div class=\"mw-heading mw-heading2\"><h2
